ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u12	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour, on behalf of the Committee on Energy and the Treasury, to present a report by the Committee on Energy and the Treasury on the draft law amending the Energy Law and certain other laws, print No 653. Ladies and gentlemen! We are dealing with an extremely important legal act which amends the Energy Law and some other laws, implementing a number of systemic objectives in terms of regulation of the fuel and natural gas market. These changes relate to different fuel and gas market institutions, but their common denominator is to organise and introduce greater transparency in the gas and liquid fuels market. First of all, these changes include: system-ordering of the liquid fuel market, increasing state control of the oil and gas intervention stock system, ordering the natural gas market, extending the obligation to maintain mandatory natural gas stocks and introducing a new legal form for the implementation of this obligation, so-called. a ticket agreement, as well as the elimination of many legislative shortcomings in the existing legislation in this area, which raises a number of interpretative doubts. In this field, unfortunately, there is a very bad situation. The characteristics of regulation of the liquid fuels sector in Poland are the dispersion of regulations in many legal acts. They are not consistent, and the consequence of such a dispersion is the lack of a uniform system. Unfortunately, such uncoordinated legislation makes it important that there is no coherent system of regulation of economic activities and control over the performance of public-law obligations related to the production, marketing and storage of fuels. The shortcomings of existing legal solutions in the field of liquid fuels in Poland, including the lack of sufficient powers to supervise them for various public administrations, in particular for the President of the URE, are a factor generating various activities by private entities operating on our market that circumvent the law. We have such a situation that, unfortunately, many start operating in the shadow economy, avoid taxation and at the same time shift costs to those who act honestly in this market. Therefore, the purpose of this law is to remove the pathologies that occur in this market in a fundamental manner. And as for existing practices, it can be said that these shortcomings in the fuel market may have resulted from the fact that there is a very strong lobbying of those who reached decision-makers, which resulted in two standards actually on the Polish fuel market: those that were valid for companies with a majority share of the State Treasury, those that were carrying the burden of securing warehouses in which they had to store fuel for difficult times, i.e. those that were cost-bearing and at the same time there were others who avoided this obligation, using legal gaps. Perhaps even these legal gaps were not a coincidence, but they were intended to play lobbyists, on whom, you can say, previous governments have been looking through their fingers for many years. Ladies and gentlemen, there was also such penetration of various fuel companies into the world of politics. One of the heads of one of the fuel chambers is one of the most famous and prominent politicians of the Civic Platform. I'm talking about Mr. Jerzy Buzek here. Perhaps this is the answer to the question of why the previous team didn't particularly want to come for the normalization of this market. But, ladies and gentlemen, during the work on this bill, we worked in a way, you can say, quite reliable, we have tabled several dozen amendments. I have these amendments here, and I'm not going to discuss them because it's quite complicated matter. Many of these amendments were clear, legislative, not fundamentally changing the intention - actually not only in principle, not actually changing the basic assumptions of the regulation of this market, sealing it and imposing equal obligations on all operators in this market. I would like to thank all the Members who have worked for hours on this bill very warmly. I particularly wanted to thank Mr Sitarski, who made an interesting contribution to this bill. This shows that the opposition can work on laws and present solutions that are needed. As rapporteur - because it is the responsibility of the rapporteur not only to present amendments, but also to say a few words, for example, about the atmosphere that existed during the drafting of the bill - I can tell you that we have actually been dealing with substantive work, with the work at which the government, the Parliament's legislators, referred to this law in a substantive manner, working on the preparation of the records, and for this I would like to thank everyone very warmly. Ladies and gentlemen, in connection with this report, I ask the High House to support this proposal. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u20	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Seym! I am pleased to present the position of the Modern for the amendment of the Energy Law Act and other laws. In fact, the novel represents significant changes primarily in the Wrestling Act. The project envisages, as the previous speakers said, extending the addressees of this obligation, the obligation to hold mandatory gas stocks to small and medium-sized enterprises, which, I will remind you, have not yet had such obligations, and the introduction of a new legal formula for the implementation of this obligation, so-called. Ticket agreement. The explanatory memorandum indicates that the proposed amendment aims to remove regulatory gaps that lead to so-called "market." gas leakage outside the borders of the Republic of Poland. The reason for this is, as the applicant points out, the conclusion of transactions of gas sales to large customers before the point of entry into the Polish transmission system, excluding the jurisdiction of our country, including the provisions of the Wrestling Act. Let me remind you that the existing laws grant exemptions from the obligation to hold such stocks to those energy companies whose number of customers is not more than 100,000. and whose gas imports do not exceed 100 million m3 during the calendar year. The energy minister is currently exempt by an administrative decision. From the date of entry into force of the amendment, if the High Sejm deems it appropriate to adopt this law, the maintenance obligation will be absolute. In our view, this will affect smaller entrepreneurs, for whom holding stocks will be a very heavy burden. Moreover, these companies will have to provide financial guarantees in the concession process for the proper performance of their duties. The latter guarantees are of particular importance for the LPG market. Let me remind you that only about 15% this fuel is produced in the country, the rest comes from imports. The novel introduces an incomprehensible requirement for a security of PLN 10 million which will have to be established by these companies. There are about hundreds of such small and medium-sized Polish companies operating in the market, employing a lot of people, and there are different estimates in this area, but it is about 40,000. persons, therefore there is a risk that these companies will either significantly reduce their activities or even disappear from the market, which would be dangerous for the market, for customers, for LPG final customers, and I would remind you that transport in the country is largely based on this fuel. The draft amendment contains transitional provisions which, in our view, interfere with the powers acquired by energy companies. In accordance with the project initiated and not completed before the date of entry into force of the Law of Procedure on the granting of an exemption from the obligation to hold stocks will expire on 30 September 2017. We are presenting important amendments, especially on LPG market operators, but also some important changes in mandatory stocks, which I will no longer discuss in detail. If these changes are accepted, we will look kindly at this law, and if they are rejected, in particular those concerning the essential issue of LPG, we will have to vote against this solution. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u44	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I present a report by the Committee on Culture and Media on the Government's draft law amending the law on copyright and related rights. You got this project in print 546. The project was sent to the committee on 20 May this year. On 9 June, its first reading took place and on 6 July a detailed examination of the project took place. Since the first reading took place in committee, a few comments are due to the High House. The aim of the project is to adapt the Copyright and Related Rights Act 1994 to Council Directive 93/83/EEC of 1993 on the coordination of certain principles concerning copyright and related rights applicable to satellite transmission and cable retransmission. The project concerns cable retransmission. The Directive generally introduces the principle of mandatory mediation of collecting societies for the management of copyright between retransmission entities, namely radio or television stations, and authors of copyright, while the Directive makes an exception to the reissue of their own radio or television programmes by cable, i.e. such retransmissions, whose reissuer owns copyright rights, including licensing rights. With us, there is no such exception to the rule. And that should be changed. It is quite urgent because the European Commission has long been pressing for the implementation of these changes to our law and has even threatened to initiate proceedings for the lack of this implementation. The project as a government has undergone a wide public consultation with radio and television broadcasters, major cable operators, as well as collecting societies for copyright management. The revision in its essential part is one-tasking, very short, and in essence there is no doubt, but it has raised considerable controversy or discussion at the level, the consultation stage, as well as the work in committee. These doubts concerned e.g. the possibility of including these exceptions, i.e. the withdrawal of intermediaries, collecting societies for the management of re-emissions via satellite or online. The interpretation of the deadline for the transfer of copyright required clarification. A lot of doubt was also raised by the interpretation of the term "translation," a term that does not exist in our legal system, rather the term "works." This argument for words, of course, has its consequences. Since the famous phrase or magazine, we know that single words can make a difference. This dispute comes mainly from the problems of interpretation of the legal terminology used in our EU law and legislation. We must ensure that our legislation correctly implements EU directives to be consistent with the Directive and with the law in force in other EU countries, as this law also has cross-border implications. During the committee's work, taking into account the amendments tabled and by the social side, and by the Legislative Office of the Sejm, a compromise proposal presented to you in print no 566 was drawn up. The Commission, without a vote of opposition, recommends that the High House adopt this proposal. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u45	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would listen to the five-minute statements on behalf of the clubs and the circle. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u46	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The governmental bill amending the Copyright and Related Rights Act aims to implement the article. 10 of the Directive of 27 September 1993 on the coordination of certain principles relating to copyright and related rights applicable to satellite transmission and cable retransmission as regards rules for the conclusion of contracts for cable remission. It is therefore intended to fill the gap in the previous incomplete implementation of this directive into Polish law. This adjustment is delayed by more than 10 years. Perhaps because, as stated in the Supreme Court's opinion on this project, it concerns one of the most difficult problems encountered in the practice of dealing with copyrights, which is to regulate the way in which rights for cable re-emissions are acquired. That Directive only requires reissue of works on the basis of an agreement concluded with the competent collecting society for the management of copyright and related rights. It therefore introduces mandatory mediation of collecting societies, excluding, in principle, the possibility of direct contracting between the reissuer and the copyright holder. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 10 provides, however, for an exception to the principle of compulsory mediation of collective management organisations in respect of rights, from which the radio and television organisations benefit in respect of their own transmission, whether or not the rights in question belong to that organisation or have been transferred to it by another copyright holder and/or related rights. According to the current wording of the Article. Article 21 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 The Act on Copyright and Related Rights to Cable Network Operators may be re-issued on cable networks only on the basis of an agreement concluded with the relevant collecting society pursuant to Article 1 of the Act on Copyright and Related Rights. 92 and Article 92 OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 671. 1 of this law is also applicable to artistic performances, phonograms and videograms. The government project provides for the introduction into the article. 21 of the new provision of paragraph 21 of the Act. 1, according to which compulsory mediation of collecting societies will not apply to the extent indicated in Article. 10 directives. The proposal therefore implements European Union law. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will support this project. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u47	Thank you very much. On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform by Mr Bogusław Sonik. You're welcome, sir.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u48	Mr. Marshal! Minister! The Directive requires re-emissions to be carried out only on the basis of an agreement concluded with the competent collecting society for the management of copyright and related rights. It introduces mandatory mediation of collecting societies, excluding, as a general rule, the possibility of direct contracting between reissuers and the copyright holder. In Polish there is almost such a duty, so this is the principle that operates in Poland. On the other hand, the Directive provides for an exception to this principle in respect of the rights which radio and television organisations enjoy in relation to their own transmission, whether or not the rights in question belong to that organisation or have been transferred to it by another copyright or related rights owner - it is Article. 10 directives. The Polish law so far did not provide for such an exception, so there was a need to amend this law. This revision is not intended, it is worth stressing, imposing a specific way of managing their rights to radio and television organisations. In this respect, they will have the freedom of procedure and either be able to grant re-emission licences themselves, or they will continue to be able to entrust their rights to the management board of the competent collecting society, as they are now. According to today's law, the cable operator is not able to contract re-emissions directly with radio and television organisations, whereas the 1993 Directive expressly requires Member States to introduce an exception to this principle, as we are doing with this amendment. This is particularly important if the rights to re-emit such works are exercised by that radio or television organisation and are exercised by it without the mediation of collecting societies, including under a licence. The essence of the proposed solution is, in accordance with the Directive, to enable the broadcaster, radio or television organisation to exercise its own right to re-emission the work and related rights in its own programmes. It is worth noting that during the committee meeting, as well as during meetings with copyright professional organisations, other issues were raised for the regulation of this law, while the legislator stated that this matter was the most urgent and that this must be changed in the first place, and therefore there is a need to continue this in the near future and to introduce further legislative changes in this matter. The Civic Platform Club will support this amendment proposal.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u52	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! This is another attempt to implement the Satellite and Cable Directive in this regard, according to the information contained in the public consultation - the sixth. On the one hand, the European Commission is threatening to initiate proceedings in the absence of implementation, and on the other hand the Directive was adopted in 1993 and its provisions do not correspond to market realities. The Commission has launched a revision of the Directive and it is possible to substantially change its content in a short time. Currently there is a mandatory mediation of collecting societies in the event of re-emissions - the dissemination by another entity than the original one - by cable, radio and television operators in any situation, so even if one entity loses the right to re-emission the work to which it has copyright, the latter must pay for its re-emission of collecting societies. According to the amendment, there will be no need for collective management organisations to pay for the remission, the payment can be made directly to the holder. It should be stressed that this is currently happening in practice. The possibility of legal approval of such action is criticised by the associations of creators, which stress that this leads to the deprivation of remuneration of those creators for which the organisations are fighting. The Directive and the proposed provision concern re-emission only by cable. Voices appear, and the Modern fully supports them, that similar legislation should concern re-emissions in other forms - either via satellite or online, especially since European law does not prohibit this, it is called. technological neutrality. The advantages of the proposed law: of course, the implementation of the directive, the absence of a threat from the Commission, which is natural, including financial risks, the break-up with the monopoly of collective management organisations in situations where a contract can be concluded directly with the entitled one, less dispute over rates to collective management organisations, simplification for radio and television operators. The downsides of this project: disputes regarding the adoption of the transfer of rights, whether it contains a licence under the directive and the law, may lead to confusion in the application of the rules, lack of compliance by the legislator on other issues raised by the participants in the public consultation, e.g. concerning the use of mediation of collecting societies to non-cable technologies, the risk of losing the protection of creators' remuneration by misusing collecting societies. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u54	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The bill is implemented in the Polish legal system art. 10 of the 1993 European Union Directive on Satellite Transmission and Cable Reemission. We have been obliged to fully implement the Directive since the entry into the European Union. There is no doubt, therefore, that it is necessary to align the provisions of Polish law with EU law, and due to the twelve-year delay in this area, it is necessary to act quickly. The proposed regulations aim at introducing an exception to the principle of compulsory mediation of collective management organisations in the reissue of works into the Polish law on copyright and related rights contained in the directive. This exception refers to the rights from which the broadcasting and broadcasting organisation uses in relation to its own transmission, whether or not the rights are owned by that organisation or transferred to it by another copyright and/or related rights owner. In shaping this amendment, the drafters were guided by the wording of the Directive. However, the wording of own transmissions used in the proposed provision raises doubts. In the absence of the word "transmitting' elsewhere in the Copyright Act, it may raise reservations and lead to different interpretations. The intention of the projector is to bring Polish law into a rapid alignment with EU law and to remove the consequences of non-compliance. A faithful translation of the directive seems to be the best solution at this point. However, I hope that the work on this bill will not be abandoned and that it will soon be changed. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u61	Thank you very much, Minister. Since the list of Members has already been exhausted, the discussion is closed.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u62	The aim of the proposed law is to implement EU law, i.e. Council Directive 93/83/EEC of 27 September 1993 on the coordination of certain rules concerning copyright and related rights applicable to satellite transmission and cable retransmission. The Directive introduced in the EU a requirement for re-emission of works to be carried out only on the basis of an agreement concluded with an appropriate collecting society for the management of copyright and related rights. It therefore introduced the so-called. compulsory mediation of collecting societies, excluding, in principle, the possibility of direct contracting between the reissuer and the copyright holder. However, the Directive provides for an exception to this principle with regard to the rights from which the radio and television organisations benefit in respect of their own transmission, regardless of whether the rights in question belong to that organisation or have been transferred to it by another copyright and/or related rights owner. The Polish law does not provide for such an exception and it is therefore necessary to amend it accordingly. The PSL Club will not vote against the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u63	We will proceed to the third reading of the bill in the voting block. We proceed with the examination of item 3 of the agenda: the report of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry on the Government's draft Act on Access to Genetic Resources and the sharing of benefits from their use (prints Nos 644 and 721). I ask Mrs Teresa Nolas to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u64	Thank you kindly. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Commission's report on the Government's draft law on access to and distribution of the benefits of genetic resources relates to prints Nos 644 and 721. The project concerns the introduction of regulations ensuring control of the use in Poland of genetic resources obtained from countries that have legally regulated access to these resources. According to the Nagoya Protocol, the project defines the tasks and characteristics of individual public administrations. This is a government project addressed to the Sejm on 20 June 2016. The Minister for the Environment has been authorised to present the position of the Government on this matter. The project was preceded by a broad public consultation with 123 stakeholders and was therefore widely consulted. The aim of this project is to bring the legislation into line with European Union legislation, as the proposed law concerns Regulation No 511/2014 on the Nagoya Protocol on access to genetic resources and the fair and fair sharing of the benefits of the use of these resources. The Nagoya Protocol was adopted to clarify this objective. It creates an international system to ensure benefits for countries providing genetic resources - both financial and non-financial benefits - and to prevent practices from getting rich at the expense of countries with high biodiversity. Both the project and the Convention on Biological Diversity apply to all genetic resources except those of human genetics. They therefore refer to the resources of plants, animals and micro-organisms, both terrestrial and aquatic species, and apply to both wild and domesticated species. Genetic resources play an increasingly important role in many sectors of the economy, especially in the food, forestry, pharmaceuticals, cosmetics and even in the field of biological energy sources. The restoration of degraded ecosystems and the conservation of endangered species play an important role. Genetic resources understood as a collected and stored stock of samples and related information form the so-called. collections. Collections are created primarily as material for research or scientific work. With regard to the Nagoya Protocol, these collections play an important role within the control chain of origin of the product concerned. The implementation of the Nagoya Protocol on the obligations of users of genetic resources is governed by Regulation No 511/2014 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 April 2014, and is called Regulation No 511/2014. This draft law sets out the tasks and characteristics of public administrations regarding the implementation of the Regulation, as well as the principles and procedures for carrying out checks on the use of genetic resources. The draft also clearly sets out the principles of liability for infringement of Regulation No 511/2014. According to the draft law, the Minister of the Environment is responsible for the implementation of the Convention on Biological Diversity and the Nagoya Protocol and will act as a national authority. The tasks related to the control of the users of genetic resources have been entrusted to the body of the Environmental Protection Inspection, and the various articles of the Act describe and exchange in detail those competences and the supervision of the authorities assigned to them. The proposed bill is rightly limited to the creation of the necessary system for controlling the use in Poland of genetic resources obtained from other countries where appropriate regulations on access to these resources are already in place. This law therefore implements the provisions of the Nagoya Protocol and allows access to genetic resources. The first reading of the project was held on 6 July. On 7 July, the draft was discussed at a joint meeting of the Commission: Health and Agriculture and Rural Development. Both committees assessed the project positively and recommended further work on the Environment and Forestry Committee. Yesterday, July 18th, a committee meeting took place in connection with this. The Commission did not comment on this project except for two minor drafting adjustments concerning Article. Article 9 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 and Article 4 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council [3] shall apply mutatis mutandis. Paragraph 24 4. The adjustments requested were accepted by the government party. The project is compatible with European Union law. The Minister for the Environment has drawn up a draft regulation for this law on the specific scope of user control, also in line with EU legislation. In conclusion, on behalf of the committee, I ask the High House to adopt this report and to adopt the act at third reading. Thank you kindly.

In order to clarify this objective, the Protocol from Nagoya to the Convention on Access to Genetic Resources and the Fair and Equal Sharing of the Benefits of the Use of Those Resources, known in short as the Nagoya Protocol, was adopted in 2010. This protocol, like the Convention on Biological Diversity, applies to all genetic resources within national jurisdictions, except human genetic resources. Thus, it concerns the resources of plants, animals, micro-organisms, both terrestrial and aquatic species. It also applies to wild and domesticated animals. However, I would like to say that yesterday's meeting of the Environment Committee was quite lively, and unless we comment on the whole project, which is, of course, the implementation of the EU directive - these solutions need to be introduced into our law - our many doubts raised during the committee's work were about penalties. Main ground of appeal it's a very strict approach to these institutions. Units of scientific institutions for the conservation of genetic resources and commercial units using these resources, namely pharmaceutical, cosmetic, biotechnology, food processing, fuel and plastics production, are placed on the same footing. We note that the principle of proportionality, which is very clearly raised several times in Regulation No 511, should be applied. She tells us about the flexible and very proportionate application of penalties and sanctions. We think that this provision should, however, be more clearly highlighted in your project, thus giving the possibility of quite mild treatment of research institutions which, for failure to comply with reporting obligations, receive quite high penalties, up to 50,000. Once again, I ask the Ministry to examine these records. There's time to correct them. On behalf of the Platform Club, I request further work on the project. Of course, we are still in favour of the project, and our support will depend on the amendments, on what you will change in the provisions on the clear division of institutions and the commercial use of our genetic resources. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u72	Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! The proposed law is the implementation of EU Regulation No 511/2014 on the Nagoya Protocol on access to genetic resources and fair and fair sharing of the benefits of the use of these resources. That Regulation shall apply together with Implementing Regulation (EU) 2015/1866. Genetic resources are a kind of support for natural capital and the basis for food security and also play an increasingly important role in many sectors of the economy, in particular pharmaceuticals, cosmetics and biological energy sources. They are essential in restoring degraded ecosystems and protecting endangered species. As genetic resources, the law recognizes materials containing functional units of heredity. These may be samples derived directly from the environment or from so-called man-made so-called samples. collections, i.e. botanical gardens, zoos, banks and microbiological collections of cultures. The Nagoya Protocol is a new instrument of international law implementing the third objective of the Convention on Biological Diversity, adopted in 2010. It is intended to ensure that countries providing genetic resources can benefit from their use of the benefits they deserve. The bill introduces, or actually creates, a system to control the use of genetic resources acquired in Poland from countries that legally regulated access to them in accordance with the Nagoya Protocol, as well as introducing fines for non-compliance with the law. Each second article of the Act constitutes a monetary penalty. As a club, we also have doubts that the Act does not distinguish between commercial operators and scientific, research entities. Indeed, most penalties can be imposed as a result of failure to make an appropriate statement, and this adequacy seems quite questionable. The Polish People's Party will not vote against this law. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u90	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I was honoured to present to the High House the report of the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs on the draft law amending the Act on Professional and Social Rehabilitation and the employment of persons with disabilities and certain other laws. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The bill in question entered the Sejm on 3 June this year, print No 564, and regulates the following problems. The Act of 27 August 1997 on occupational rehabilitation requires sheltered employment establishments to create a rehabilitation fund. This appropriation is intended to cover the financing of professional rehabilitation. Art. Article 33 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 4a indicates the penalties to be applied if the fund's money is used in breach of the law, and nothing about sanctions when the funds are not transferred by the work establishment to that fund at all. Hence, the proposal to introduce provisions on the equal treatment of the incompatible misallocation of funds of the Fund with total non-transfer of funds. The proposal also proposes a change in the composition, organisation and mode of action of the National Consultative Council on People with Disabilities. In addition, the amendment proposes to amend the Sign Language Act of 19 August 2011 concerning the requirements to be met by the Chairman of the Mig Language Council. In addition, it is proposed to postpone the deadline for vacathio legis rules on the issue of new disability cards. June 22nd this year The first reading took place in the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs. At the request of the applicants, given the high interest in this novel and the importance of matter, it was submitted to the Permanent Sub-Committee on Persons with Disabilities. A number of amendments were adopted by the congratulating subcommittee on 5 July. It is more important to clarify the provisions on the terms of office of the National Consultative Council on Disabled Persons. In this case, a limitation to two 4-year term for members of non-governmental organisations was adopted. A broad board was adopted, the principle of representativeness, i.e. five representatives of state administrations, five representatives of local self-government units, were taken into account of representatives of employers' organisations, representatives of a representative union organisation within the meaning of the Social Dialogue Council Act and 20 representatives of non-governmental organisations. A record has been deleted that candidates on the board cannot be employees or members of organisations supporting them. The provisions concerning the dismissal of members of this Council have been clarified. Importantly, provision has been made to allow for the payment of cash claims or the remission of interest due on arrears. In addition, the provisions on qualifications to be held by the chairman of the Sign Language Board have been improved. The minimum requirements in this context indicate a qualified person who knows Polish sign language and Polish language. The deadline for the entry into force of the Disabled Persons Card was also corrected and set here on 1 August 2017. The report of the subcommittee was adopted by the Social Policy Committee without amendments on 6 July 2017. The report on the bill's novel is in print No 583. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Therefore, on behalf of the committee, I request the adoption of the attached bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u92	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice on the parliamentary draft law amending the Act on Professional and Social Rehabilitation and Employment of Persons with Disabilities. Print No 564 entered the Sejm on 3 June, and on 6 June was directed to work in the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs. On 6 July, the committee adopted a proposal recommending that the High House adopt a draft law. The project introduces solutions to improve the functioning of bodies representing persons with disabilities in their cases and regulates the rules on the misuse of funds from the rehabilitation fund for persons with disabilities. In view of the overly restrictive rules on penalties for employers operating sheltered work establishments who have not transferred funds to the fund, applicants have proposed to reduce the financial burden imposed on the employer by obliging him to pay 30% of his contribution to the rehabilitation fund. the amount of unallocated funds and not, as yet, 30% of the total amount of funds collected in the share fund. In addition, the amendment amends the provisions on the functioning of the National Consultative Council for the Protection of Persons with Disabilities. The powers of the Board, which will become an advisory and advisory body with the possibility to appoint experts, commission research and expertise in carrying out its tasks, are being extended in accordance with the new provisions. The project also clarifies the number and rules of membership of the Council of representatives of non-governmental organisations other than those mentioned in the Act and the procedure for notification to its composition of representatives of local self-government units. The Act guarantees membership of 20 representatives of other non-governmental organisations appointed no longer than two terms of office of the Council, with the support of at least 15 non-governmental organisations acting in favour of persons with disabilities. These solutions aim to increase the competence and transparency of the board. At the same time, the scope of the Council will be extended to which the applicant imposes an obligation to present an opinion to the representative of the Government on the performance of the tasks set out in the Act and to issue 30 days of opinion on the documents submitted to the Council by the representative within a deadline of 30 days. This provision is also intended to safeguard the proper performance of statutory obligations. According to Art. Article 43 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 Paragraph 4 of the Act will lose the membership of the Council of persons sentenced to a final sentence for a criminal offence committed intentionally, as well as those who are deviating from their duties and unable to fulfil them for health reasons. The new law shall delegate competence to the proxy for persons with disabilities to convene meetings of the Council. These changes will undoubtedly increase the efficiency of the body dealing with the issues of persons with disabilities and ensure that groups with different types of disability are properly representative. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 49f applicants shall enter provisions allowing the remission, distribution into instalments or deferral of the payment of claims relating to undue financing for the remuneration of disabled workers, together with the possibility of redemption of interest in cases justified inter alia. important public interest, social considerations or other reasons which deserve to be taken into account. The employer of disabled people also receives a guarantee of liquidity financing by the payroll fund and social security contributions during the period of arrears. This is an important solution that takes into account the well-being of disabled workers. The project also includes amendments to the Sign Language Act as regards the conditions to be met by the President of the Polish Foreign Language Council. To date, this function could be performed by a person with an education or experience at the same time in terms of knowledge of three ways of communication. The applicants prefer to relax this strict requirement and reduce the qualification of the candidate for the chairman of the board to learn Polish sign language and Polish language, which will undoubtedly enable more candidates to apply for this function prepared for it. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The proposed amendment to the Act clarifies, improves and, as a result, makes the current mode of action of the National Consultative Council for Disabled Persons more transparent, better safeguards the rights of workers entitled to use the State Fund for Rehabilitation of Persons with Disabilities, meeting the social expectations in this area, which is why the Law and Justice Club will support the adoption of the proposed bill by the High House. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u127	We will proceed to the third reading of the bill in the voting block. We proceed with the examination of item 5 of the agenda: First reading of the Government draft bill on the rules of settlement in the tax on goods and services and the reimbursement of public funds for projects financed by the budget of the European Union or by the Member States of the European Free Trade Agreement by local government units (print No 709). I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Finance of Mr Leszek Skiba to present the justification for the project. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u128	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft bill on the rules of settlement in the tax on goods and services and the reimbursement of public funds for the implementation of projects financed by the budget of the European Union or by the Member States of the European Free Trade Agreement by local government units is a government project. The main objective of the proposed law is to provide local government units with legal instruments supporting the centralisation process and to reduce the burden of centralising VAT settlements in local authorities in connection with the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union of 19 September 2015 in the case of the municipality of Wrocław. In the Court's view, the municipal budgetary units do not bear the economic risk associated with their economic activities on behalf of and on behalf of the municipality or are liable for or liable for the damage caused by those activities because they do not have their own assets, do not earn their own income or bear the costs relating to such activities. The revenue generated is paid to the budget of the municipality and the expenditure is paid directly from the budget. The consequence of the VAT ruling is the absence of a tax separation of local budget units, which means that all activities carried out by them in favour of third parties should be settled by the local government unit which created them and the activities carried out between budgetary units are internal. The Court's judgment did not concern the local units of budgetary establishments, but the Supreme Administrative Court adopted a resolution on 26 October 2015 in which it adopted, in relation to the local budgetary establishments, a similar interpretation as the Court of Justice in respect of the local budgetary units. Consequently, the resolution adopted by the court must be considered that local government budget establishments, as well as local government budgetary units, do not have a tax separation under the VAT rules, so that all their activities in favour of third parties should be accounted for by the local government unit which set them up and the activities in the local government, both between individuals and establishments, are internal. In view of the Court's judgment and the NSA's resolution, it is necessary to centralise VAT settlements in local governments as soon as possible. This centralisation must cover the organisational units set up by the local government, which are budgetary units, budgetary establishments and municipal offices, district old offices and marshal offices. The concept adopted by the proposed law assumes the compulsory centralisation of the accounts forward, i.e. from 1 January 2017, so that the execution of the Court's judgment can take place with the provision of adequate time for the local authorities to adjust the accounts. The Minister of Finance has already informed local authorities about this date in its communication. In order to support local authorities in the process of centralisation, it is envisaged that the resources allocated to their tasks should not be reimbursed to the extent that there has been a change in the eligibility of the tax in the part relating to the amount of input tax financed by the funds allocated to the implementation of the projects. The draft submitted also assumes that there is no need to centralise past accounts made prior to the Court's decision. If a local authority wishes to exercise the right to calculate the input tax in connection with this judgment, it will be able to make adjustments to the VAT return, starting from the earliest period for which the adjustment will be made, which will be decided by the local authority, until the time of centralisation, taking into account the limitation periods. However, the condition for using the calculation is that, in accordance with the Court's judgment, the complex corrections take account of the accounts of all business units and cover all settlement periods not covered by the statute in which the local government accounted as separate from its business units for the taxable person, starting from the earliest accounting period for which the adjustment was made. The provisions proposed in the draft are expected by the local government, as they will guarantee the units of local government and their organisational units, which, until the Court's judgment was given, have been treated as separate - under the VAT rules - taxable persons of this tax, the certainty of VAT settlement. To this end, the proposed law provides for the succession of rights and obligations, which will ensure the entry, as from the date on which the local government unit takes joint settlement with its organisational units, into the rights and obligations of those entities, including with regard to past accounts. This means among others. no need to issue corrective invoices, as the local government unit will also acquire the right to deduct from the invoices issued to its organisational units by entering into the rights and obligations of its units. The proposed law also provides for the possibility for organisational units to benefit from the exemption until the date of the joint clearance of the so-called exemption. where, at the date of the judgment of the Court of Justice, they have benefited from such an exemption. This will also apply to organisational units whose settlements have already been centralised with the local government unit before the date of entry into force of the draft law. The centralisation process also requires a resolution of the question of the registration of sales by the self-government unit to natural persons not engaged in business with the use of registering banks. The centralisation of accounts would imply the need to exchange all existing fiscal cash registers used by the local government and to purchase new fiscal cash registers for entities which have so far benefited from exemptions from trading records. In order to minimise these costs for local authorities, it is envisaged that trading may continue to be recorded through existing cash registers whose fiscalisation has been carried out by organisational units, but no longer than 31 December 2018. This regulation will exempt local authorities from the obligation to exchange registers or their fiscal memory and will enable trading to be recorded by existing cash registers. At the same time, the proposed law maintains until 31 December 2016 the exemption of local government units from the obligation to keep records of turnover and amounts of tax due by means of registering banks for the activities of those organisational units which benefited from such exemption on the day preceding the date of centralisation. Failure to introduce such rules would imply the need to buy new fiscal cash registers for all the organisational units that have benefited from the exemption so far. In view of the fact that centralisation may give rise to the need for a large number of registers to be purchased by the local government, it is justified to extend exceptional participation by the State budget in the cost of purchasing these registers, guaranteed in the current legislation only for the cashiers purchased at the time of the obligation to keep records, to start registration. The proposed law will also introduce adjustments to the Corporate Income Tax Act in respect of the non-calculation into the revenue of local government budget facilities received from the budget of the local government unit of financial resources resulting from the settlement of VAT relating to that undertaking and in the Public Finance Act which will allow the transfer of amounts to the budget establishment when the local government unit receives a refund resulting from the tax return of the excess tax charged over the due amount. When presenting the bill, I ask the High House to support and direct it to work in the Committee on Public Finance. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u130	Thank you very much. Dear Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, it is my great honour and pleasure to present our club's opinion on the Government bill on the bill of settlement in the tax on goods and services and the reimbursement of public funds for the implementation of projects financed by the budget of the European Union or by the Member States of the European Free Trade Agreement, contained in print No 709. The bill submitted concerns all local government units, such as municipal offices, district old offices and marshal offices, as well as local authorities' organisational units and budgetary establishments. The proposed changes are designed to make compulsory centralisation of VAT settlements in local governments, whereby the local government unit will be obliged to settle VAT together with all organisational units, which will no longer be able to account separately as from 1 January 2017 at the latest. The proposed amendments result from the need to comply both with the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union of 29 September 2015 and with the resolution of the Supreme Administrative Court of 26 October 2015, which clearly indicated the conditions that public agencies must fulfil in order to be a VAT taxable person. In addition, the Supreme Administrative Court found that, despite the greater degree of autonomy of the budget establishment than the budgetary units, it was not sufficiently independent and did not fulfil many conditions. - it cannot be regarded as separate from the municipality of the VAT taxable person, and all activities carried out for the benefit of third parties should be settled by the local authority which created them and the operations carried out within the local government of the units and budgetary establishments are internal in nature. The arguments cited oblige the centralisation of VAT settlements in local governments, and the proposed bill responds to these needs and provides legal instruments to the local authorities both to support the centralisation process and to minimise the nuisances involved. Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Given both the case law of the Court of Justice of the European Union and the position of the Supreme Administrative Court, the Law and Justice Club recognises the need to implement the proposed regulation and will vote in favour of it. Thank you very much.

This is justified by Article. 173 of Directive 112: the proportion of deduction shall be determined for all transactions carried out by the taxable person under paragraph 1. 2 [[♪ The bell ♪]] States may require the taxpayer to determine the proportion of deduction for each sector but not for each unit. I know that the explanatory statement is that it can be understood that way. It cannot be understood that way, especially if English and French are taken into account. There's no reason that this only applies to individuals. I'm out of time. I have a few more remarks, but I'll report them to the committee. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u135	Thank you very much, sir. I ask Mrs Genowefa Tokarska to speak on behalf of the Polish People's Party Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u136	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, I will present my position on the government's draft VAT bill - I will no longer read the whole long name - from print No 709. In short, the draft law concerns the centralisation of VAT settlements in local governments. The proposed amendments result from the decisions of the Court of Justice of the European Union and the resolution of the Supreme Administrative Court. Previous VAT clearances were carried out independently by the units and budgetary establishments set up by the local government unit. These units were treated as separate taxpayers. The Court of Justice of the European Union has ruled that bodies governed by public law, such as municipal budgetary units, cannot be regarded as taxable persons for VAT because they do not meet the criterion of independence. Therefore, local government budgetary units should be accounted for by the local government unit which created them. The centralization consists of organisational units, i.e. budgetary units, budget bets, as well as municipal offices, district old offices and marshal offices. According to the draft law, the centralisation of the accounts will be carried forward. However, the local authorities will be able to decide to correct the accounts. Taking into account the centralised model, the municipality accounts for VAT as a taxable person. Self-governments themselves will decide what period to cover with a centralised billing model by making adjustments to the tax returns for all periods in which the business units settled separately the tax, counting from the earliest settlement period for which these adjustments were submitted, until the centralized model was introduced, of course taking into account the statute of limitations. It will not be possible, which is obvious, to make selective adjustments, i.e. only for the settlement periods chosen by the local authorities. According to the draft law, this mandatory centralisation of accounts will take place from 1 January 2017. By the end of this year, 2016, local authorities will not question the settlements carried out using the current billing model. In conclusion, it should be stressed that changes are necessary. They stem from the overriding law, from the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union of 29 September 2015 in Case C-276/14 of the Municipality of Wrocław v Minister of Finance and the resolution of the Supreme Administrative Court of 26 October 2015. However, I would like to express my concern. It is a pity that we have no opinion on these proposed specific solutions on the part of the directly concerned, i.e. local authorities, their organisations or associations. There is not even an opinion on the part of the Joint Government and Government Committee. The Minister said today that local governments are looking forward to these solutions. Indeed, there have been numerous controversy over the settlement of VAT by the municipalities, but we lacked this document here. Taking all the above into account, the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party is in favour of transferring the project to the committee. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u146	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Thank you so much for the questions. Indeed, the law is very complicated, because it concerns many aspects and also takes into account the different states that some local governments will be centralised in the sense of settlement, some have already voluntarily centralised, some are not yet centralised and are planning to do so before 1 January, autumn. So these are additional elements that make the bill complicated. In general, I'll start with the timeline. There were questions precisely why 1 January, and here I wanted to answer the question of the Member. Self-governments were involved, i.e. We are in constant contact with local governments, local authorities unions, municipalities in this regard because the operation that will be carried out is indeed very complicated. Here I answer Mr Wójcikowski's question immediately, why it cannot be a little later. The local authorities themselves pointed out that from their perspective it was optimal on 1 January due to the accounts and that the introduction of this in the middle of the year was quite difficult for them. The deadline for 1 January was therefore chosen. Of course, 1 January 2017 is from the point of view of this process a possible term, we know from local governments, because this law does not surprise them, there was a discussion with the joint committee, there were communications, information and there is also a great interest in this issue, which we see in the Ministry of Finance, because local governments also have here. additional questions. It really shows that this process is going on in some way. Of course, it is not simple, we are aware of it, and we will try as much as possible to support the work of the local government, so that this process is as simple as possible. At the same time, we see that in the case of local governments of very large cities, e.g. M.S. Warsaw, which has more than 1000 units, this centralization has succeeded. It shows that this process is to be carried out. At the same time, we are aware of the possible need for support or clarification and, if necessary, we can declare support in this respect so that this process of centralization and interpretation of the law is as accessible to local government units as possible. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u148	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Seym! On behalf of the applicants, I have the honour to present the parliamentary bill amending the Aviation Law Act in print no. 710. Firstly, the aim of the proposed amendments is to clarify the existing rules on the organisation of the State Commission for the Investigation of Aviation Accidents and to ensure that they comply with European Union law. Secondly, we want to simplify the procedures for converting airports to exclusive use at public airport and thus ensure greater and equal availability for stakeholders, which will have a positive impact on competition and can have a positive impact on airlines. It is worth noting at this point that simplification of bureaucratic procedures will not adversely affect the safety of air operations, as the new category of non-certified public use airports will not reduce safety requirements - they will be the same as for exclusive use airports. And experience shows that these are enough. To discuss the details of the proposed amendments, I would like to stress that, in accordance with the article. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1 Regulation (EU) No 996/2010 of the European Union requires each Member State to ensure that a safety investigation or investigation, i.e. accidents, incidents in civil aviation, is carried out by an independent and specialised organisation. Meanwhile, the current article. Article 17 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 of the Aviation Law provides that the State Commission for the Investigation of Air Accidents shall carry out its tasks on behalf of the Minister responsible for Transport. This provision in our project was removed because it raised doubts about the independence of the committee. In addition, in the draft article. Article 17 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 We have made changes to replace the current model of appointment of committee members for their appointment and dismissal. This mechanism was derived from the solutions adopted for the State Commission for Railway Accidents Investigation. We have also included provisions in our project to guarantee the independence of the members of the State Air Accident Investigation Commission and experts involved in the work of the committee. This solution gives them the right to express their views freely as part of the studies, studies, analyses, opinions and reports that have been prepared. On the other hand, amendments to the Article. 17a paragraph. They shall comply with the requirements of Directive 2003/88/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council concerning certain aspects of the organisation of working time of committee members. They were granted the same powers as the members of the State Marine Accident Investigation Commission. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 2 of the draft law contains a provision whereby the working relationship with the members of the committees designated under the existing provisions expires 30 days after the entry into force of the law if they are not appointed as members of the committee before that date. In the event of termination of their employment, they will be entitled to a payment of 3 months' salary. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! A key change in this draft law, and long-awaited by the aviation environment, is the amendment of paragraphs. Article 7 54 and the addition of paragraph 54. 7a, 7b and 7c in this article. The essence of this change is to replace the non-functioning temporary opening institution of an aerodrome of exclusive use for public use with a new practical category - non-certified public use airports. Air Law has been in force for 5 years, theoretically allowing for a temporary change in the availability of an exclusive airport to public use, but in practice it has not been applied because of the need to meet all the conditions required of an airport for public use. The procedure for permanent conversion of exclusive use aerodromes at an aerodrome with limited certification has also failed because it is very complicated and time-consuming. Its element is the change of the airport manager. The procedure takes between 6 and 10 months, and during which the new manager must demonstrate that he has similar documents as when setting up an airport. This is completely unnecessary because this procedure applies to an operating aerodrome registered in the civil airport register. In addition, the applicant is examined financially on a similar basis as in the case of large airports, while the financial situation of the local airport manager, even after changing its availability, will not affect the level of safety. Applications do not have here also issues of business protection, business activity, because there are no scheduled air services at local airports and therefore carriers who would be at risk of loss in the event of a possible insolvency of the manager. It is also unnecessary for the President of the Civil Aviation Authority to supervise the setting of airport charges in respect of airports with limited certification and uncertified. The need to comply with these bureaucratic requirements has resulted in only three public-use airports in Poland with limited certification, while in the Czech Republic there are over 40 and in Germany approx. 160. It is therefore necessary to adopt the solutions proposed in the draft law, which will allow for an efficient change of airport availability to the public according to the will and needs of the manager, who will be free to choose - to operate the airport as exclusive or as public. In Poland we have more than 40 airports of exclusive use, so the facilitations in converting them into public ones, which we propose in our project, will lead to a levelling of disparities between our communication network and other countries of the European Union. As I mentioned at the beginning of my speech, the technical and operational requirements for non-certified airports will be similar to those for exclusive use airports, which guarantees that the level of safety at the new category airports will not be reduced, while the procedure for changing the availability of the airport carried out according to our project will be simple to avoid several months of administrative proceedings, and will also remove many unnecessary bureaucratic tasks from the President of the Civil Aviation Office. Another flexible solution that our project introduces to meet the expectations of the aviation environment is the abolition of the mandatory requirement to provide airport air information service, so-called. AFIS. Its maintenance is very expensive for the airport manager. The new wording of the provision in Article. 120 will allow the managing airport to designate an AFIS or a flight manager who will be responsible for providing information to incoming and departing aircraft. In conclusion, the parliamentary draft amending of the Aviation Law will contribute to the independence of the members of the State Commission for the Investigation of Aviation Accidents and will introduce favourable regulations on the organisation of working time for them. The draft law meets the expectations of the aviation community to make airports public, ensuring equal and free access to them by all interested parties. It is an important step towards the re-bureaucratisation of aviation activities and will also have positive social and economic impacts. I therefore recommend that the High House refer the project to the Committee on Infrastructure and then adopt it. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The increase in the number of airports that can accept aircraft on a similar basis to public airports will have a positive impact on competition in the air services market and will increase the interest of airlines. The current legal provisions require the authority issuing the authorisation to manage the public airport to analyse mainly the financial situation of the applicant. So, Art. 61a, as proposed, will not adversely affect air traffic safety in terms of simplifying the procedure for applying for a change in airport accessibility characteristics. The procedure is to be simplified and accelerated and its limitations and requirements result should only be of the necessity to maintain air safety. The amendment also aims to simplify the procedure for changing the airport manager. In the current legal situation, as a result of an unjustified reference to the rules on the change of airport manager to Article. Article 55 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 The law governing the setting-up of an airport requires a complex and long-term procedure to be carried out by a legally effective change of the managing entity of an airport. The applicant shall be required to submit a set of up-to-date documents to the same extent as an airman's assumption, often having to request decisions on environmental conditions and building conditions, even if they are actually issued in the same condition as the one in force at the time of the change to the manager. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u252	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Last year it was 15 years since the Institute of National Remembrance was established. This time was an opportunity for us to get acquainted with many opinions about our work, including those which flowed from representatives of both chambers of the Polish Parliament and representatives of other authorities of the state. Above all, however, it was a time of normal hard work of the Institute of National Remembrance. This is the work which carries out the mission of the Institute, expressed and defined in the preamble to the Act on the Institute of National Remembrance, which states that Parliament created an institute with a view to preserving the memory of the great amount of victims, losses and damages suffered by the Polish people during and after the Second World War, the patriotic traditions of the struggles of the Polish people with the invaders, Nazism and communism, the actions of citizens in favour of the independent existence of the Polish state and in defence of freedom and human dignity, the obligation to prosecute crimes against peace, humanity and war crimes, and the duty of redressing by our country to all those who have been wronged by the state violating human rights. At the same time, it was believed that no unlawful action by the State against citizens could be protected by secrecy or forgotten. And for 16 years now, on this mission, we carry out the tasks put forward by the Institute of National Remembrance. The forms of these tasks have changed many times, the mission remains the same. One of the most important tasks placed before the Institute is to restore justice in a literal sense by investigating crimes from the period of World War II and from the time of the communist dictatorship. As we know, this scope has now been extended since the entry into force of the amendment. 1078 investigations were completed in 2015, of which more than 60% related to communist crimes, the others - in most of the German crimes from the period of World War II. It is worth noting that in recent years the number of prosecutors working vertically in IPN has decreased by about 1/5, but the number of proceedings remains similar. Last year, 14 indictments against 16 people were sent to the courts. And it is worth noting that this is another year in which we come out of a certain breakdown in the number of indictments brought to the courts following the May 2010 Supreme Court resolution introducing a new way of defining the limitation period for communist crimes, which resulted in the remission of hundreds of cases, many of which had the chance to go to the courts together with the prosecutions. However, it is also worth noting that it is not only the acts of prosecution that define the meaning of the investigations conducted by the Institute of National Remembrance. It should be recalled that our prosecutors are also obliged to investigate when there are coded conditions for redemption, such as the death of the perpetrator. And even this legal act, which is the decision to terminate the investigation, contains a legal definition, an indication of the legal qualification of the crime, where possible, an indication of its perpetrators, and, most importantly, an indication of the victims. It is in the case of very many crimes from the past the only legal act, an act of the Polish State which refers to them. And I am convinced that not only today, but also in the future it will be of great importance. There are investigations that are of particular public interest. I'll just mention one thing. Last year, the Institute of National Remembrance was involved in many forms in commemorating the 70th anniversary of the Augustów manhunt, the biggest post-war crime committed on the Polish nation. The investigation also plays an important role here. And although we have no longer had a chance for legal aid from Russia for many years, we are still experiencing progress in knowledge. Last year, prosecutors obtained an expert opinion based on historical aerial photographs and modern satellite photos to identify places where the victims of the Augustow raid are most likely buried. Unfortunately, the most important of them are already outside Poland and this verification will require the approval of the Belarusian party. I would also like to mention that the actions of prosecutors constitute and will continue to form the basis for scientific work for many years. The minutes of witness hearings alone, of which there are several tens of thousands - last year it was over 4,000. Interrogated witnesses - create a huge material for further research. And last year, an important publication was published based on an investigation into the assassination of the Holy Father John Paul II entitled "Agca was not alone." Prosecutors of the Institute of National Remembrance are also involved in the search for victims of communist terror, which, unfortunately, I see should be recalled, started in 2011 with the cooperation of other institutions and, above all, the Council for the Protection of Remembrance and Martyrdom and the Ministry of Justice. And in 2012, we started a project to identify the found remains. Last year most of the victims were found in Bialystok. And it can be said that the prison garden in Bialystok is such a symbol of Polish history, because looking for victims of communist terror, we also found hundreds of victims of German terror from World War II. In total, 385 bodies have already been found there. Search work also lasted in Opole, at the Brodnowski Cemetery in Warsaw, in Płock, in Gdańsk, in Rzeszów, in Tarnów and elsewhere. In many of them, they led to the discovery of the remains of the victims. Last year, a law entered into force that allowed the later graves to be moved from such places. First of all, we are talking about the Warsaw Link. On the first day that this law entered into force, the Institute submitted applications for the transfer of all graves from Ł1 and Ł2. These applications have already been completed. The process of moving these graves begins, which will end exhumations and later also identify the victims. In total, after 5 years we can talk about finding more than 800 victims and identifying 66 of them. This is another task of the Institute of National Remembrance. Last year, the process of judging was dominated by local elections, including those from 2014 and last year's, parliamentary and presidential elections. In total, 68 thousand were made available to the Mirror Bureau as a result of these elections. new illustrative statements, which once again increased the gap between claims that are already verified and those that are awaiting verification. Last year, 9460 claims were verified, i.e. Approximately twice as many as 5 years earlier, 133 requests for the initiation of a mirror procedure were addressed to the courts, as well as 6 posts in the so-called. self-image cases. In 168 cases, final judgments were brought, including 124 declaring that the contested illustrative statements were incorrect. It is worth remembering that the Bureau of Mirrors are not only prosecutors and not only repercussions of illustrative statements, but also the creation of four powerful online catalogs, which serve the transparency of Polish public life: the catalog of the oppressed, the catalog of the officers of the communist security apparatus, the catalog of the so-called. a party-state apparatus from before 1989 and finally a catalogue of people currently performing the most important public functions. Last year, 12,500 people appeared in these catalogues. new entries. The largest part of the Institute of National Remembrance is the archive. The archive resource of the IPN already counts over 91 km.b. the act. Last year it increased by 450 m.b. Here the question may arise as to how it happened that 15 years after the institute was established, our archives are still growing. Well, still in a lot of institutions, at the moment this concerns mainly police, prisons, partly courts, prosecutors' offices, and voivodeship offices, we discover documents that are being transferred to the Institute of National Remembrance. Also in the Seym Archive last year, such documents were found or rather we managed to obtain them after long efforts. They relate to so-called. the committee of Darkniewski and the Committee on Constitutional Responsibility investigating the matter of martial law. We also take over private collections, including important emigration collections. I will mention that last year we took over, which in fact equals saving from destruction, the collection of Prof. Stanisław Bóbr-Tylingi and Prof. Anna Maria Cienciły. Our archive also collects, in the form of electronic copies, materials from foreign archives, documents that relate to the history of Poland and Poles in the 20th century. Last year we received such copies from Estonian, Georgian, American, Ukrainian and Hungarian archives. In total, over the last five years, we've been able to raise 650,000. That kind of copy of documents. The law, which has entered into force, abolishes the so-called. but the Institute did not wait for this moment of liquidation and in 2015 this collection was reduced by 6819 archival units, which is the best result of its reduction in the entire history of the Institute of National Remembrance. It is worth mentioning that we managed, with cooperation, among others. with Internal Security Agency, read 263 tapes that have been stored in this collection so far and the contents of which we did not know. 2015 was another period of development of the digital archive system, which we started to create in 2012. It is currently the most modern archival information system in Poland and at the same time the archive management system. At the moment, we are already technologically prepared to make documents available remotely to authorized persons. Of course, there is still a planned digitisation of the resource, which is carried out in two basic directions, i.e. saving documents particularly damaged, as well as making digital copies of these documents, which are of the utmost interest to users, so that more people can benefit from them at the same time. For example, the scale of our archive's activities gives information that 65 thousand were realized last year. different types of applications for access to documents. It is worth remembering that the Archive of the Institute of National Remembrance has received new statutory tasks in recent years. These include the drafting of applications for the award of the Cross of Freedom and Solidarity. The IPN is the only institution of the Polish state that can ask the President of the Republic to award this award, which exists only five years, but it already enjoys a huge reputation and an estimate among former anti-communist opposition activists. Last year, we made 882 requests for broadcasting, and we also organised 30 celebrations during which I had the honour of giving these decorations on behalf of the President. Last year, another new task was to implement the Act on opposition activists and repressed persons. Here 735 administrative decisions were issued during the first months of its validity. The archive serves the entire Institute of National Remembrance, but in a special way its contents are used by employees of the Institute of National Remembrance, who of course also use other archives. The Institute's research work is carried out within 11 central research projects and 14 branch projects. We also carry out two major documentary projects in cooperation with NGOs. It is an index of Poles repressed for helping Jews and a project on personal losses and victims of the German occupation. In addition to the witness statements I mentioned earlier, our educational section also carries out the activity of recording in-depth notations with witnesses of history. Last year, 173 of them were carried out. The effects of scientific research are presented during the conference. Last year we implemented more than 50 scientific conferences and more than 20 popularization conferences. Educational activities are probably the form of the implementation of the IPN mission, which is most widely observed, but this is also due to its extremely wide range. It is enough to mention that 35 thousand participated in only one project, the lessons from the latest history in 2015. students. From the new forms we are introducing, I would like to mention the Oxford debates.
