Further forms of our educational activity are, for example, a project called "History in Colors" aimed at the youngest. For many years, because of the importance and seriousness of the topics we are dealing with, the Institute has not had its offer for the youngest. We've had her for a few years. Last year, a special educational package was issued within the framework of the project "Colored History," which ordered 14,000. schools and kindergartens, which shows the popularity of our offer and, on the other hand, the great demand for historical education Or at the same time patriotic education at this most basic level. I would like to mention educational projects such as the memory stones, and this cannot be forgotten... - meetings with women who have been through the hell of camps and camps, I will tell you about free Poland. Several hundred project groups, each composed of several students, participate in these projects each year. I pay particular attention to this project or these projects because they allow us to read history in an unusual way. It can be said that, as part of these projects, students, of course with substantive support, discover the past themselves. They meet, talk to witnesses of history, seek documents and often even discover stories that have been unknown to researchers so far. But the most important thing is that it affects that they have a completely different relationship with the past, a completely different, deeper understanding of the importance of the past for our present and for our future. Of course, we also perform various types of competitions, including nationwide ones such as.Equipments and buttons with rust eagle. The Institute, as you know, has been seeking for many years to change the patrons of streets, squares, bridges that are connected to the communist system. Thanks to the law passed this year, this process will be completed within the next year. For some time now we have added this element: we are showing which people should be patrons of the streets. And that's the name of the project where we published 30 brochures that hit, among others. to the inhabitants of specific streets. Because this is also a dimension of education, this time also aimed at adults, to build this connection with history by knowing who was a patron or who is a patron of some street. We are expanding our Truth portal about camps dedicated to German concentration camps and German extermination camps in Poland. Two new language versions of this portal were created last year. Exhibitions prepared by the Institute of National Remembrance continue to be a great success. We decided to use this brand to meet the need for meetings with history in many places in Poland. These are, of course, not such fixed points, because we cannot guarantee their service, but on selected days of the month there are always initiatives of the Institute of National Remembrance in specific places. It is worth saying that such a wide educational activity of the IPN is also possible thanks to the fact that we can count on the support of many social partners. Education is used to know the past and thus to understand the present. It is used to build memory, especially the memory of those we owe it to, about the heroes of Polish history. But it is worth noting that through knowledge and memory this education also serves to build attitudes. This education also brings important values from the past to our modern life. Let me say that this is not only true for young people. I think we should all, especially those of us who perform public service, look at the memory of the past as a commitment in our service today. The Institute of National Remembrance last year published 195 publications in a traditional, or paper version. In addition, in our digital library we placed 40 publications in an electronic version. The international activity of the Institute of National Remembrance covers several areas. The first is the dissemination of knowledge about Polish history outside the country. Poland is seen as a country that has developed a model solution on this issue, and last year we shared this experience, among others. with representatives of Albania, Tunisia and Ukraine. Each year, together with the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, we organize the Warsaw Dialogue for Democracy conference, which is also an important element of the reference to Polish experience. The fourth element is multilateral cooperation. The Institute of National Remembrance was a co-founder and is an active member of two international organizations: the European Network of Archive Institutions, dealing with secret services files, and the Platform of European Remembrance and Conscience. We actively cooperate in joint efforts to show above all our experience, the experience of Central and Eastern Europe in the 20th century, i.e. the experience of two totalitarianisms. The last element is bilateral cooperation. Last year, cooperation with the Hungarian National Remembrance Committee, which was recently established, was particularly active. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u254	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Mr. President! I present the report of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights held on 6 July 2016, which was the subject of a report on the activities of the Institute of National Remembrance in 2015. I would like to stress that the work of the Institute of National Remembrance is very comprehensive. The works concern archival activities, concerning historical documentation, this latest history of ours, concerning research and science, publishing, investigative activities, illustrious matters or finally, as also mentioned by the President, the broadly understood international activity. My report will cover only the most important aspects of this activity and will deal with what was at the committee meeting. As the President presented the work of the Institute of National Remembrance in 2015 very comprehensively in his speech today, it is impossible to avoid certain repetitions here. First of all, the President at the beginning of the speech and at the committee meeting pointed out that it was the year of the 15th anniversary of the Institute of National Remembrance. As far as archival resources are concerned, these resources continue to increase each year as further institutions provide documents that should be in the resources of the Institute of National Remembrance. Recently, documents from private resources have also been adopted. These were mostly documents relating to immigration activities. Currently, the liquidation phase is so-called. It's a restricted collection. It reduced by nearly 7,000. units. Importantly, the Institute of National Remembrance has been able to read the content of parts of magnetic tapes that have been stored in a proprietary collection so far. It will then be possible to make them available to the public. The activities of the former anti-communist opposition activists are linked to the entry into force of the relevant law, which was still pending in the previous parliamentary term. The Institute of National Remembrance is the only legal institution authorised to conduct proceedings concerning the award of the Cross of Freedom and Solidarity, and is the only institution empowered to submit applications to the President of the Republic for this award. The IPN also granted in 2015 the status of anti-communist opposition activist, issued 735 administrative decisions in this area, recognized 613 requests for evidence of opposition activity. This is also necessary for the granting of various powers, which are the responsibility of the President of the Office for Combatants and Repressed Persons. On average, more than 1 000 prosecutors of the Institute of National Remembrance lead every year as far as criminal prosecutions against the Polish nation are concerned. investigations, although the number of prosecutors in recent years, as the President also mentioned, has been reduced by 20%. In 2015 14 indictments were sent to the courts, prosecutors of the Institute of National Remembrance last year questioned more than 4,000. Witnesses. The number of indictments decreased following the resolution of the Supreme Court of May 2011, when the Supreme Court redefined the limitation periods for certain categories of communist crimes. This resulted in the remission of most of the investigations due to the fact that there were also such cases that the perpetrators died. However, it is important that the circumstances of death have been established, especially for those close to their victims. The IPN prosecutors also participated in the process of searching for victims of communist terror. The remains of 385 people were found not only in the post-war period but also by the German occupier. IPN also applies for the transfer of graves, among others. on the Warsaw Link. This year's amendment to the Act on the Institute of National Remembrance introduced new provisions concerning this type of procedure. As far as educational activities are concerned, they took very different forms. I will mention educational projects such as the Polish free Poland - these are meetings with people who have passed the hell of camps and victims, educational project - Lessons from recent history - 35 thousand participated in such lessons with the employees of the Institute of National Remembrance. students, these are meetings often attended by direct witnesses of events and their relations. Each year there is also an exhibition activity - 186 exhibitions from previous years were exhibited, and in 2015 there were 20 new exhibitions. There was also a special activity due to anniversaries - the 75th anniversary of the Katyn crime and the 35th anniversary of the establishment of the Solidarity. All of this also involves the activity of IPN on the Internet and cooperation with TV. Activities outside the country - here too IPN is very active. The Constitutional Tribunal stated in its ruling a year ago, considering the legal question of one of the district courts, that it is necessary for a judge to be held liable for a illustrious lie to waive immunity by a disciplinary court. And this judgment has caused a lot of dismissal of proceedings against judges - the illustrious liars. However, more than a year ago, the Constitutional Tribunal issued such a ruling that it should first be the waiver of the immunity of the judge. Many proceedings have therefore been terminated. Of course, it doesn't close the way for this procedure to lead again, but disciplinary proceedings are very long-term, there is the first and second instance. These are disciplinary courts that make up the judges themselves. We know that these procedures often take a very long time for various reasons. We proceed with the examination of item 9 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law on trust services and electronic identification (print No 713). I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Development of Mr Tadeusz Kościński to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u294	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I present a draft law on trust services and electronic identification aimed at ensuring the conditions for implementing eIDAS, Regulation No 910/2014 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 23 July 2014 on electronic identification and trust services for electronic transactions in the internal market, by: point 1 - adapting the national legal order to comply with the eIDAS Regulation by amending the laws inconsistent with the EU Regulation, including the repeal of the existing e-signatation law; point 2 - regulating the areas identified in the eIDAS Regulation, specifying at national level, and in particular the regulation of supervision of trust services; point 3 - introducing changes to the entire legal system in connection with the harmonisation of terminology at European level, including. by replacing the concept of secure electronic signature verified by an important certificate qualified by a qualified electronic signature. This law will have the following significant effects: supervision of trust services, including e-signatures, will be transferred from the Ministry of Development to the Ministry of Digitalisation. The national procedure necessary for the notification of electronic identification means shall be introduced. Administrative and criminal sanctions for qualified trust service providers will be regulated. Work on adapting the national legal order to the eIDAS regulation is being carried out in stages due to a narrow delegation to the adoption of this law, as well as the duration of the eIDAS Regulation. The eIDAS Regulation sets out definitions, safety levels and legal implications for a number of trust services in the field of Community law. The major changes taking advantage of the new opportunities provided by eIDAS in public administration will be prepared this year in the framework of another legislative act aimed at developing and seizing trust services in public services. Many issues, such as: Regulation of electronic service, including administrative, civil, judicial and fiscal procedures, requires in-depth consultation with both potential suppliers and public operators and service users. The coordinator of this area will be the Minister of Digitization. The eIDAS Regulation is directly applicable in the national legal order from 1 July this year. It should be borne in mind that EU legislative and standardisation work on delegated and implementing acts in connection with eIDAS continues. Technical and organisational changes related to the alignment with the eIDAS Regulation are currently being implemented in all Member States. The most important is the implementation of recognised qualified signatures, stamps and time stamps from other Member States. The eIDAS Regulation and the draft law ensure the unification of trust services in the European Union. By submitting this bill, I am counting on the support of the High House. Thank you kindly.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u295	Thank you very much, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would listen to the five-minute statements on behalf of the clubs and the circle. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Witold Czarnecki, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u296	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice to present to the High House the position of my club on the government draft law on trust services and electronic identification along with draft implementing acts, Parliamentary printing No 713. 3 April 2014 The European Parliament has adopted Regulation No 910/2014 of the European Parliament and of the Council on electronic identification and public trust services in relation to e-commerce in the internal market. This regulation operates under the short name eIDAS regulation, from electronic identification and trust services. At the outset, I would like to stress that the regulation is an act which directly and uniformly regulates a particular problem across the European Union, unlike a directive, which is only a Community legal framework for a particular problem. The eIDAS Regulation repealed Directive 1999/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council as from 1 July 2016. 93/EC of 13 December 1999 The aim of the eIDAS Regulation is to increase confidence in electronic activities between citizens, businesses and public authorities, thereby contributing to the development of online services, e-business or e-commerce in the economic area of the European Union. Today, citizens of EU countries cannot make full use of these services because electronic identification systems are not recognised in other Member States. The eIDAS Regulation lays down the principles of mutual recognition of electronic identification means and the principles of cooperation, including in the regulated area, between Member States of the Union. The current legal solutions in Poland do not agree with the regulation mentioned above and therefore it is necessary not only to repeal the Act on electronic signature, but also to refrain from seeking national solutions, except for the issues identified in the Regulation as being within the competence of the Member States. The issue of safe electronic signature and time marking has been regulated in Polish law so far. The government bill introduces into the Polish legal system a whole range of new concepts and necessary changes in ordering such a state, such as to ensure the consistency of the Polish legal system. The Trust Services and Electronic Identification Act follows the eIDAS Regulation by introducing a closed catalogue of trust services such as electronic seal, electronic time stamp, e-service service or authentication of websites, although some of these services are partly regulated but providing the legal basis for issuing the necessary implementing acts. Finally, I would stress that the Act assumes that the supervisory authority for trust services will be the Minister responsible for IT, although the existing structure based on the National Certification Centre remains within the national trust infrastructure. [[NCC]] operating within the National Bank of Poland. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the government draft law on trust services and electronic identification. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

As regards Mr Galemby's question, what will be the effect of introducing an additional definition of a mining company into the Mining Act, I have mentioned this a little earlier. Like I said, it's about the ones before 2007, so mostly Dębiensko, Jaworzno, Siersza. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u393	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Group of Members, I have the honour to present a draft act on the remuneration of the managers of state positions and on the amendment of certain other laws. Maybe I'll start with how it is today. [[Game in the O.R., bell ringing.]] And today, we have a basic salary in the form of twice the average salary in Poland plus a minimum salary in a given year, plus a function supplement. Today is the fact that we have frozen for many years valorizations. Today's the day we have no incentive system. High Izbo! This is not a law on salary increases for members of government administration, this is not a law on increases for parliamentarians, this is not a law on increases for ministers. In this act we propose some innovative solution to make wages dependent on the degree of wealth of Poles. We propose that four factors be taken into account: firstly, GDP growth, secondly, Gini's ratio, thirdly, average wages in Poland and fourthly, minimum wages. As a result, if this were to be introduced, the principle of linking remuneration with efficiency and economic growth will apply. In the event of a crisis or incompetent governments, these wages may even be lower than they currently are. A decrease in GDP growth of less than 2% is sufficient. I will remind you that this was the case in Poland, that in 2012 it was barely around 2%, in 2009, 1.6% , between 1.2 and 1.4% between 2001 and 2002. I want to talk about this law as much as possible in substance, without the unnecessary publicist discussion that sometimes takes place here, so let me talk about the most important assumptions and briefly discuss the whole act. Ladies and gentlemen! If we decide to do so with the current rates, of course there is nothing to hide, it will result in some kind of wage increase. However, in order to achieve this, first in the companies of the State Treasury 60 million PLN managed to reduce, first. Secondly, any increase in this area for government administration has been frozen since 2008. Today, the Deputy Minister receives 7,000 as a result. net zlotys, and sometimes manage, as you know, even a dozen-milli-billion-dollar budgets. So we have a situation that this salary, to put it mildly, often causes the best professionals in the market to be very difficult to persuade to work in government. But we have an even more interesting situation. Here are department directors who earn more than the ministers supervising their work. At the moment, as many as 19 of the 20 CEOs earn more than the Minister, as many as 13 more than the Prime Minister, and one more than the President. July 15th this year In Opole there was a funeral ceremony of a soldier of independence, a steadfast Henryk Wolski, ps. Fox. A loving husband and father, an extraordinary patriot, fought for us, for our future. He was one of the cursed soldiers who was willing to give his life in the name of the good of the nation and future generations. A hero that Communists would rather forget. A steadfast man, for whom the struggle for better Poland was a duty not only to his homeland, but also to future generations, who only today will learn the truth about who they were, whose traces and memories have been deliberately wiped out over the years. In times like these, no words will be able to give away the great pain that most of us feel today when we hear about the death of the dead. Mr Henryk Wolski, for whom his love for his mother was always superior. Henryk Wolski, son of Kazimierz and Genowefy, was born on 24 July 1930 in White Bor in the district of Grudziądz. Henryk Wolski grew up in a family in which the most important values were always God, honor and homeland, and such values throughout his entire life were guided. The fight for Poland has been with him since the early years. The most beautiful and carefree years of his childhood were taken from him by the Nazi occupation, while the joyous years of his youth were filled with a difficult period of communist regime. Unfortunately, he was not able to graduate from Pedagogical High School in Grudziądz because in March 1949 he was arrested and sentenced to 10 years of imprisonment for participating in a criminal organization of an armed nature fighting the regime. Henryk Wolski during this period was the commander of the underground organization of the National Army of Foxes. The organization led by Henryk Wolski was active in Grudziądz, Świecie and Chełma in 1948. This organisation was governed by the independent student organisation Mon. The Student Resistance. Armed resistance against communist slavery by the Fox was the cause of his arrest. As a political prisoner he was imprisoned in Grudziądz, in Sztum and in Wronki. He was very humiliated and tortured there. He was released from prison in February 1955 with an order to leave his residence. Henryk Wolski was sent to the other end of Poland, to Opole with a ban on contacting the nearest family. In Opole, he meets his future wife Emilia, starts a family, but his life is far from stable. He was still being harassed by the security services. Henryk Wolski was awarded the National Army Cross in 1995 and was awarded the Veteran Walk for Freedom and Independence medal and the Anti-Communist Union of Political Prisoners in 1999. In 2000 he was appointed lieutenant and in 2002 he was promoted to lieutenant. Henryk Wolski was very proud that he could wait for the time of honoring the cursed soldiers. His wish was to be able to wear the uniform of the Home Army soldiers again. Ladies and gentlemen, may our memory of him be an expression of great gratitude to the sleeper. Henryk Wolski for all the merits for Poland and Poles, for the daily joy and sense of humor that characterized him. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Everyone who knew him remembers good times and beautiful memories. Colonel Wolski, we will never forget you. Glory to the hero! [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u433	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! Divorce is one of the most traumatic situations in the life of children and adults. It always carries the risk of serious dysfunction in the functioning of the child. It's not just stress and negative emotions, it's also changing many aspects of life. The child has to face difficult situations, often moving to a new place, changing the organisation of time or spending holidays. For many years now, in the West of Europe, Scandinavia, the USA and Australia, the courts have given parents alternate care. The rule was to leave the parenting power of both parents. Polish law does not explicitly regulate the issue of transversal care, but the provisions of the family code are so formulated that it is possible for courts to rule on it. It is a condition that both parents agree on a proposal, expressed in agreement on how to exercise parental authority and maintain contact with a child, so-called. It's a parenting plan. Generally, in Poland it is the rule to entrust only one parent with full parental authority, which to some extent limits the authority of the other parent. The standard distribution of child care after divorce is that the child stays with the mother, and the father has the right to take care of the child one, twice a week for 2 hours, every other weekend, during part of the world, holidays or winter holidays. In practice, it appears that judicial decisions are not being enforced. Mothers often make it difficult or impossible to contact their children with their father or vice versa, fathers do not take care of their children and do not execute judgment. There should also be mention of problems related to the collection of maintenance obligations. The purpose of the decision is to have both parents full of parental authority. Replacement care is the most suitable for the child and guarantees the interests of both parents by maintaining a relationship with the child. In the case of care, the parenting authority is always entitled to both parents, and the place of residence of the child is determined by both the mother and the father. In addition, parents should live close to each other or at least in the same city, so that the child can attend one kindergarten or school. Moreover, parents should be able to communicate with one another so that the court can positively assess the prognosis of their cooperation in matters of the child. Parents should guard the welfare of their children. Each parent has a child for half a month, and there is no need to pay child support. The current legal framework strengthens the conflict between parents. This conflict is manifested by a long struggle for children, mutual talks of conflicted parents, whether about abuse or harassment, which leads to long-term court trials. In a situation of disagreement between parents concerning the care of children, the courts entrust children to one parent by restricting the parental authority of the other. Mostly, because in 97% cases, care is taken by the mother, whether or not there is a legitimate concern about the execution of a court order concerning the father's dealings with children. I would like to appeal to parents who should stand up to the task and, above all, take care of the child's mental well-being. We all know how difficult this is in the face of divorce. In addition to ensuring as much emotional security as possible, parents need to establish technical aspects of child care after divorce, sometimes causing many problems. I encourage parents to take turns. Replacement care is a technical solution, a week or two with mom, a week or two with dad. Parental equivalence is a more complex concept, because it is not about organizing time, but about establishing cooperation and equal involvement of both parents in caring for the child. A well-functioning alternate model is based on equivalence, which assumes mutual respect and a friendly atmosphere provided to the child. In equivalent parenting, there is no place to manipulate the feelings of the child, to criticize the other parent in the presence of the child, to alienate, to affect the negative influence of third parties on the relationship of the child with the parent so-called. Second camp, jealous of the baby's love. And there's no room for comfort, no place to dump a baby that's gonna feel like an unnecessary suitcase. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Replacement care is to serve the child, not the parents. Ladies and gentlemen! Replacement care is a valuable way to solve the child care problem in the event of his parents' divorce. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u437	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! On 3 July 2016, the 95th anniversary of the Volunteer Fire Service in Pacierzów was held in the municipality of Kłomnice. The fire in the village of Pacierzów dates back to the early 20's. In the 20th century, when several people who felt called to help others, founded in 1921. Volunteer Fire Department. The beginnings of the fire brigade formation were difficult. Thanks to the generosity of the residents, a wooden firehouse was established in the centre of the village, one of the first in the municipality of Kłomnice. Initially, the fire unit had little, but thanks to its strength and professionality, volunteers from Pacierzów were among the best in the municipality. Over time, more and more experience was gained, more and more equipment was gained, which was used during fires or natural disasters, among others. during the extinguishing of the church in the pot during World War II. Already in the 1950s, the amount of equipment in the fire department was so large that they began to think of a new, larger guard. Thanks to delegations to offices and sponsors, the construction of the new house was completed in 1959. The following years have been conducive to the development of the guard, new equipment has been obtained, and the building has been expanded with a view to obtaining a fire car. The first average fire extinguisher was purchased from its own resources from the Railway Fire Service in Tarnowskie Góry in 1980. Since then, firefighters from Pacierzów have been called to most events in the commune and surrounding areas. In 1991, the inhabitants funded the flag for the unit of the TSO, which still serves today during church celebrations, firefighters, but also represents their history. For the past several years, the fire department has expanded its equipment by the next necessary equipment for the operation, and the culmination of 95 years of their history was the handover of the Volunteer Fire Department in Pacierzów to a new fire-fighting vehicle. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u443	Statement on the 115th anniversary of Cardinal Stefan Wyszyński's birth. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On August 3, the 115th anniversary of Cardinal Stefan Wyszyński's birth - the Primate of the Millennium, the prominent leader of the clergy and the husband of the state, co-creators of the social changes that led to the fall of communism. Stefan Wyszyński was born on August 3, 1901 in Zuzela nad Bug. He attended the Higher Seminary in Włocławek. He was ordained priestly on 23rd birthday - 3rd August 1924. Stefan Wyszyński was a professor of sociology and canonical law at the Higher Seminary in Włocławek. During the Nazi occupation he was a chaplain of the National Army and insurgent hospital in Laska. When the war was over, Wyszyński became the rector of the Higher Seminary in Włocławek. In March 1946 he became a bishop of Lublin. On 12 May, on Jasna Góra, he accepted the sacrament with the bishop, and in his bishop he placed the words "Sola Deo" which means "only God." In 1948. Stefan Wyszyński was appointed Archbishop of Gniezno and Warsaw and Primate of Poland. On 12 January 1953 he was incorporated by Pope Pius XII into the College of Cardinals. As Cardinal, he participated in four conclaves: 1958, 1963 and August and October 1978. During the communist repression against the Church, Stefan Wyszyński defended the Christian identity of the nation and initiated an agreement with the communist authorities. The government guaranteed among others. teaching religion in schools and the existence of KUL and theological departments in Cracow and Warsaw. The Church, on the other hand, committed itself to respect for power and cooperation. The agreement was a far-reaching compromise on the part of the clergy, and the primacy itself had many voices of criticism. However, the compromise did not correspond to the communist authorities, and the government began to interfere more with the internal affairs of the Church. So the bishops sent a letter with the famous words (we cannot) which were an expression of opposition to the submission of the Church of power. When the primate publicized the contents of the letter, he was arrested. Until 1956 he was interned in Rywałd, Stochek Warminski, Prudnik and Komańczyk. For fear of mass pilgrimages, Communists decided to move the clergyman to a new place every few months. These were years of sacrifice, suffering and isolation. However, the Cardinal's relentless attitude in defending the Catholic faith and the communist society did not manage to break. The Primate himself was seen by Poles as a martyr for faith and the Church. During the imprisonment, Cardinal Wyszyński developed a program for the renewal of religious life in Poland contained in the idea of Jasna Góra's Vows of the Nation, as well as the foundation of Great Novena before the millennium of Poland's baptism. In 1965 the Polish Episcopate under the direction of Primate Wyszyński sent a message to German bishops, which called for reconciliation. The harsh reactions of the authorities of the Polish People's Republic have triggered famous words: "We grant forgiveness and ask forgiveness." This resulted in an even greater chase of Communists for the Primate, which was expressed among others. No permission to go to Rome for the Synod of Bishops. However, every blow given to Primate Wyszynski by the Communists only strengthened his authority and motivated him to fight. Cardinal Wyszyński participated in all sessions of the Second Vatican Council. Between 1980 and 1981, he was a mediator between Solidarity and the authorities. In mid-March 1981, Wyszyński was diagnosed with cancer. He died on 28 May 1981 in the House of Archbishops of Warsaw and was buried in the Warsaw architectry of St. John the Baptist in the crypt of the bishops of Warsaw. His funeral became a national manifestation. Since 1 June 1987 Primate Wyszyński has been sitting in a specially erected chapel for him. On 3 May 1994, he was posthumously awarded the Order of the White Eagle. On 29 May 1989, the beatification process of Cardinal Stefan Wyszyński began on the initiative of Pope John Paul II. From that moment on, he is entitled to the title of God's servant.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-19-sejm-23-1.u444	I'm ordering a recess until tomorrow, i.e. On 20 July 2016, no later than 12 p.m. 9. Thank you very much. [[Adjournment at the meeting at 3 p.m. 21 min 59]]
